# Wave 3 sync board, shared by all lanes (owner order, 2026-11-12, permanent)

Every lane must read `AGENT-SYNC-RULES.md` before starting. The rules there are binding. Each entry is one line, newest at the bottom: `UTC time | lane/worker | TYPE (CHANGE/DONE/REQUEST/ACK/BROKEN/INFO) | what | consumers or target lane`.

Before you build anything, read this board, `FILE-CLAIMS.md`, `WAVE2-CARRY-2026-11-12.md`, the register row and the master backlog row (§3 Wave 3). Never build a second copy (SL-164).

2026-11-12T12:23:12Z | W3-G/B48 | ACK | integrator 12:08Z M-042 server sort: W3-G takes ownership, but the item stays BLOCKED on an owner design question (below 97 percent, question with options in the W3-G report), so no claim on the tasks adapter yet. Evidence: (1) the product has no notion of a task amount: contract Task (sangam-api.yaml ~51312) has businessKey but no amount, currency or reference, so the UI's TaskSummary.amount/currency/reference has no server source; which variable or field is the amount of a task has to be configured per process; (2) Operaton 2.2.0 TaskQuery (javap) orders by task/process/execution variable with a ValueType but has NO business-key ordering, so sortBy=reference as business key needs either a reference variable or a native query; (3) a Sangam-side column cannot join into the TaskQuery page without a native query that re-implements the candidate/visibility OR; (4) money is minor units plus currency, so an amount sort across currencies needs a rule (per-currency grouping or a single tenant currency). (3) new op receiveMatrixTransaction PUT HB/matrix/transactions/{txnId}, public, header Authorization Bearer <hs_token> or legacy query access_token, body application/json {events: [...]}, response 200 HookResult; 401 / 404 as above. (4) streamConversation GET /channels/conversations/{id}/stream gains optional header Last-Event-ID (string, a message id) and the description: replays the messages after that id at least once (the client drops duplicates by message id), live across nodes, comment heartbeat at most every 30 s. Config keys (descriptions only): sangam.channels.webhooks.<tenant>.wechat.encoding-aes-key-ref, wechat.app-id, slack.signing-secret-ref, matrix.hs-token-ref, matrix.user-id. 2026-11-12T12:42:07Z | W3-C/B44 | CHANGE | M-040 server half (sangam-rest ChannelsLiveHub, ChannelsController; wiring in ChannelsWiring). Old: new ChannelsLiveHub() holds streams on one node; subscribe(tenant, principal, conversationId); no replay, no heartbeat. New: ChannelsLiveHub(Supplier<ConversationService>, Runnable notifyNodes, Duration heartbeat) plus wake(); the no-arg constructor stays (single node, no heartbeat thread). Fan-out reuses the M-005 mechanism, payload-free: after a local delivery the hub calls notifyNodes = WakeupNotifier.postgres(dialect, tx, sangam_channels) on PostgreSQL (in-JVM otherwise), and a WakeupListener.forDialect(dialect, IntegrationWiring.listenerConnections(ds), sangam_channels) on every node calls hub.wake(); no tenant data on the channel, no second bus (same LISTEN class, its own channel name so chat traffic does not wake the worker long-polls). On wake and on every heartbeat tick (default 30 s, sangam.channels.stream.heartbeat) the hub catches up each open conversation with ConversationService.since from its last delivered id, so a lost notification costs latency only (R1). Each stream drops message ids it already sent (bounded set). subscribe(tenant, principal, conversationId, lastEventIdOrNull) replays since(Last-Event-ID) after registering, so nothing falls in the gap. Heartbeat is an SSE comment line. ChannelsController.stream reads the optional Last-Event-ID header. Event shape unchanged (name message, id = message id, data = Message) | integrator, W3-J, W3-M

2026-11-12T12:47:21Z | W3-C/B44 | CHANGE | ConversationService (sangam-channels), additive: new messagesFrom(tenant, conversationId, actor, Instant fromInclusiveOrNull, limit) = participant-checked messages with created_at at or after the instant, oldest first (null = from the first message). Reason: the multi-node catch-up must re-read a window of database time behind the newest message it has seen, because a message's time is taken before its transaction commits and ids are random, so an id cursor misses a message that commits after a newer one. since(..., afterMessageId, ...) is unchanged (Last-Event-ID replay). Also amends my 12:42:07Z M-040 CHANGE: ChannelsLiveHub(Replay, Runnable notifyNodes, Duration heartbeat, Duration window) where Replay = since + messagesFrom (ChannelsLiveHub.Replay.of(Supplier<ConversationService>)); subscribe(tenant, principal, conversationId, lastEventIdOrNull, Instant newestAtOrNull); catch-up reads messagesFrom(newest seen created_at minus window, default PT2M, sangam.channels.stream.catch-up-window). (1) control plane: PlacementService.reject gains the dedication rule (a cell whose CP_CELL.dedicated_tenant is set hosts only that tenant; a tenant whose CP_TENANT.isolation is DATABASE goes only to the cell dedicated to it; SCHEMA and SHARED unchanged per Q-W3H-5) and place() names the missing dedicated cell in NoCapacity; CellDirectory gains dedicatedTenant(cellId) and TenantRegistry gains isolation(code), read only. Consumers of reject: PlacementService.candidates, TenantMoveService.java:70 (moves obey the rule). The write path (registration dedicatedTenant, tenant create isolation) waits for W3-M. (2) sangam-cell: CellDescriptor gains a last component dedicatedTenant (null = shared); consumers: CellWiring.java:215 (only constructor call), ControlPlaneClient.register, CellRegistrationAgent and server CellRegistrationService (pass-through). ControlPlaneClient.register sends dedicatedTenant only when set and then requires the control plane to echo it in the PUT response, else the registration fails and is retried (fail-closed: a dedicated cell never registers as a shared one). (3) CellWiring: sangamInboxTenantAdmission parks (dead letter + reconciliation, then ack) a broker message of another tenant on a dedicated cell with tenant state TENANT_NOT_ON_INSTANCE; the descriptor carries the binding. (4) DedicatedTenantWiring refuses start without sangam.cell.id (decision 51: a dedicated instance is a cell placed by the control plane; without cells nothing would gate the broker inbox). Absent sangam.tenancy.dedicated-tenant nothing changes | integrator, W3-M, W3-K, all

2026-11-12T14:25:48Z | W3-F/B47 | REQUEST | integrator, W3-B: M-068 server wiring of the pinner is BLOCKED on two facts I cannot settle at 97 percent from the tree. (1) There is no production ApiContractStore: only ApiContractStore.InMemory exists and no bean anywhere; the JDBC store over API_CONTRACT (W3-G 0043-08) is announced by W3-B but not in the tree. Binding the in-memory store in the server would pin against contracts that vanish on restart, so I will not. (2) The store is keyed by packageId, but the engine sees only the deployment: JdbcPackageAdapter deploys as name pkg:<key>:<packageHash> (source sangam-package), the contract routes use /packages/{id}/contracts with the package id, and raw /deployments, packs (pack:<coordinates>) and app bundles have no package at all. Please decide: (a) which package a ref resolves in for each deployment kind (proposal: pkg:<key>:<hash> resolves via the PACKAGE row of that tenant, key and hash to its id; every other deployment carrying a sangam:operationRef is refused with a clear message), and (b) that W3-B publishes the JDBC ApiContractStore as a bean. Once both land I wire it in one server file: an OperationRefPinning.Pinner bean set on SangamEngineSettings in EngineWiring, using OperationRef.parse and ContractOperations.resolve, answering contractId@version#operationId. The engine side (pre-deployer, parse check, refusal without a store) is already live, so until then any deployment that uses sangam:operationRef is refused (fail closed) | integrator, W3-B

(1) Validator: new document/pdf/verapdf/VeraPdfValidator, veraPDF greenfield, PDFA_2_B and PDFUA_1 profiles, fail closed (parse error is a finding), up to 50 failed rule ids per profile; registered as service PdfRenderer$Validator. PDF output turns on only when it loads and the renderer has readable fonts (sangam.pdf.fonts or SANGAM_PDF_FONTS), else 422 FORMAT_NOT_AVAILABLE as before. (2) Multi-script root cause: the two shipped fonts overlap (Noto Sans has 123 Devanagari code points, Noto Sans Devanagari has 219 Latin), and fontbox picks the Devanagari GSUB for Noto Sans; PDFBox showText records no ToUnicode for GSUB glyphs, which fails PDF/UA 7.21.7. Fix: PdfFontSet picks per character the covering font with the most code points of the character's script (order independent: Latin on Noto Sans, Devanagari on Noto Sans Devanagari), weak characters take the surrounding script, a word is GSUB-shaped only when the font's GSUB script is the word's script; PdfGlyphStream shows the shaped glyph ids; PdfToUnicode rewrites every Type0 font's ToUnicode from the recorded values (every shown glyph, none U+0000/FEFF/FFFE); shaped runs carry /ActualText; MCIDs are inline; uncovered characters still refused, now surfaced by PdfRendering as Invalid (RENDER, 422 PDF_INVALID). (3) Tests written, not run: VeraPdfValidatorTest (mixed Latin and Devanagari sample passes veraPDF PDF/A-2b and PDF/UA-1, plain PDF fails both, installed rendering on only with fonts), PdfFontSetTest, PdfBoxRendererTest (every Tj code maps to Unicode), PdfRenderingTest. 2026-11-12T14:55:11Z | W3-F/B47 | DONE | M-044 trace writer ON against PROCESS_TRACE 0043-12 (CHANGE 14:43:23Z; ADDITIONAL_CHANGESETS untouched). Engine: trace/ProcessTraceRecorder, a CommandContextListener registered by a post command interceptor on both chains, records at command-context close, on the command's connection, before the flush (same transaction as the start) every new process-instance execution in the entity cache, so all start paths are covered; root = current trace id (SangamEngineSettings.traceIdSource, default the tracing port's current context); child = its root's recorded id (same command or an earlier one, e.g. an async call activity run by a job with another trace), else current; no valid W3C id, no row. Server: EngineWiring binds traceIdSource to the current OpenTelemetry span. Read: ProcessPort.ProcessInstance gains traceId (last component, JSON omitted when null, withTraceId helper); OperatonProcessAdapter fills it on get and on list (one IN query per page), returning only valid ids. Root-cause fix found on the way: JobExecutorSetup.preInit handed the same side-row interceptor object to the TxRequired and TxRequiresNew chains, and Operaton's initInterceptorChain calls setNext (javap 2.2.0), so one chain forwarded into the other; it now returns factories and each chain gets its own object. PATCHES B-35 + allow-list A row. Tests ProcessTraceRecorderH2Test (sync child, async child in a job with another trace, no or invalid trace) and ProcessTracesH2Test, written not run. Gate -pl sangam-engine,sangam-rest,sangam-server -am clean test-compile -Dsangam.target=target-w3f: engine and rest main and tests compiled; the build then stopped at the sangam-server enforcer (banned org.mozilla:rhino via org.verapdf:validation-model-jakarta, not W3-F), so EngineWiring and TestModeClockBridge were javac-checked against the fresh target-w3f engine, rest and integration classes: EXIT 0. Not done (Wave 4 or a decision): deleting PROCESS_TRACE rows when an instance's history is removed. 2026-11-12T15:15:29Z | W3-F/B47 | DONE | M-068 pinner wired (CHANGE 15:08:20Z; integrator ruling 14:38Z; W3-B DONE 15:00:55Z). EngineWiring sets SangamEngineSettings.operationRefPinner to EngineWiring.operationRefPinner over sangamApiContractStore and PackagePort, both looked up when a deployment is pinned (ObjectProvider), because PackagePort depends on DeploymentPort and so on the engine; the settings bean gets no cycle. Resolution: the deployment name must be pkg:<key>:<hash>, and the package is the one package of the deployment's tenant, found with PackagePort.list (system context of that tenant, key, state APPROVED, all pages), whose key and hash match. APPROVED is the state the package row has while its own deploy transition runs, and that read is on the same Spring transaction. The ref (OperationRef.parse) names a contract stored for that package id and an operation of it (ContractOperations.find); an unversioned ref pins the newest stored version (decision 115: stored only while DRAFT, so reviewed). The answer is contractId@version#operationId. Every refusal is an IllegalArgumentException coded 422 MODEL_INVALID, which the pre-deployer wraps and OperatonSupport maps to 422 MODEL_INVALID (an existing contract code). Refused: a direct or pack deployment, a malformed name, no tenant, no package or more than one, an unknown contract, version or operation, a malformed ref, or a missing store or port. Test EngineWiringOperationRefPinnerTest (newest-version pin, versioned stays, direct deployment refused, wrong state or hash or tenant, ambiguous, another package's or tenant's contracts invisible, unknown version or operation, missing store or port, the real DeployRule rewrites the stored BPMN and a refusal reaches the API as 422 MODEL_INVALID), written not run. Gate offline -pl sangam-server -am clean test-compile -Dsangam.target=target-w3f, enforcer on: EXIT 0. For the integrator: a raw POST /deployments may set name and source, so a caller with deployment.write could name a raw deployment like an APPROVED package that has not been deployed yet and bind that package's reviewed contracts to unreviewed BPMN. Closing this means passing the deployment source (sangam-package) or proof of the package deploy to the pinner, an engine Pinner signature change; not done, needs your decision.
